#35938. Instacart calls it quits on technology it hailed as 'the future of grocery shopping'

Instacart is carting away a technology it once said would play a key role in shopping.

The grocery delivery company will wind down the team that develops its Caper smart shopping carts, it said on Monday. The move, approved by Instacart's board of directors, will be completed by the second quarter of 2027.

A wind-down will cost the company between $55 million and $80 million, including the costs of terminating some employees and contracts, it said in a filing with the SEC. Instacart will reinvest savings from the decision into "strategic growth initiatives."

Caper carts were one of the most visible of Instacart's retail technology efforts. The company began as a delivery-focused startup in 2012, but diversified into selling technology to retailers over the last several years.

Equipped with cameras and sensors, Caper carts could weigh fruit, track what customers placed inside or removed from them, apply coupons, and accept payments when customers were done shopping. Instacart also saw an opportunity to serve users tailored ads on the carts' screens.

In early 2024, Instacart said it planned to roll out "thousands" of the Caper carts to stores. Then-CEO Fidji Simo said: "We really believe that it's going to be the future of grocery shopping."

Amazon, which makes a similar smart shopping cart, said at the start of 2026 that it planned to start using a new version at Whole Foods Market stores.

According to the global aviation analytics company Cirium, cited by Aviation Club Center, the services are set to begin on January 10, 14, and 21, 2027, respectively, using Boeing 737 aircraft. These dates are subject to change before operations commence.

The expansion follows the U.S. Department of Transportation’s decision on Oct. 16 to lift restrictions that had prevented expanded operations from AICM and AIFA. The dispute centered on the allocation of airport slots and the relocation of cargo flights to Felipe Ángeles Airport.

Aeroméxico’s three new services will bring the airline’s total to 21 weekly departures to the U.S.

#35945. EP228: How SSH Works

Strands harness is the open-source, state-of-the-art, fully assembled agent harness. It’s a complete, production-ready agent the moment you install it. Across six benchmarks it matched or beat other harnesses on accuracy while using 28% fewer tokens.

Top 6 Techniques to Make Your AI System Efficient

How do you know if your AI app actually works?

🚀 New Course: AI Evals in Practice Starts on Oct. 7

Secure shell is a method to access a remote machine securely over an unsecured network. It starts with a TCP connection from the SSH client to the remote machine (SSH server).

Both the machines exchange the SSH versions and negotiate crypto algorithms, and each side runs a key exchange protocol. The SSH server sends the public key and a signature, which the client machine verifies. That host key is then checked against the known_hosts file.

Both the machines then derive session keys on their end and do not share them over the network. Same keys, computed separately, never put on the wire. Then the SSH client starts authentication by sending the public key for login.

The SSH server matches the public key in the authorized_keys, and the SSH client also signs the auth request with the private key and sends the digital signature to the SSH server.

The private key never leaves your machine. The server will verify the signature with the client public key. This completes the SSH authentication, and now the session is open for communication.

Serving LLMs at scale is expensive. These 6 popular techniques reduce latency and cost, and make your system more efficient.

Streaming: The model sends each token as soon as it becomes ready. This changes the user’s perceived latency to time-to-first-token.

Quantization: Convert the model's weights to lower precision like FP8. This conversion leads to less memory usage, which translates to faster and cheaper serving.

Continuous batching: With this technique, new requests are added to the batch once a slot becomes available. Therefore, the GPU is less idle.

Prefix caching: This technique caches internal calculations for given input prompts. At runtime, if the prompt has a cached prefix, those calculations are skipped.

Paged KV cache: This technique stores the cache in fixed-size blocks instead of one contiguous chunk. At runtime, blocks are allocated as the sequence grows, so memory is not reserved up front.

Speculative decoding: Here, a small model (speculator) proposes multiple tokens. After that, the main model verifies them in one forward pass.

Over to you: what is missing from this list?

Agents can generate code. Getting it right for your system, team conventions, and past decisions is the hard part. You end up wasting time and tokens in the correction loops.

More MCPs, rules, and bigger context windows give agents access to information, but not understanding. The teams pulling ahead have a context layer to give agents exactly what they need for the task at hand.

Where teams get stuck on the AI maturity curve and why common fixes fall short

How a context layer solves for quality, efficiency, and cost

Live demo: the same coding task with and without a context layer

If you want to maximize the value you get from AI agents, this one is worth your time.

An AI agent can be thought of as a simple While-loop.

It uses an LLM to select an action, executes that action, evaluates the result, and repeats the process until the task is complete. Let’s take a closer look at each of these components:

Brain: The LLM is the core. It reads the situation, thinks, and decides what to do next. The big shift from chatbot to agent: the model isn’t writing text anymore, it’s making choices.

Planning: Hard tasks need more than one step. Agents break them down using methods like Chain of Thought (think step by step), Tree of Thoughts (try options, pick the best), orReflexion (learn from mistakes and retry). Planning turns a fuzzy goal into clear actions.

Tools: An LLM without tools is a brain in a jar. Tools are functions the model can call, like web search, code execution, APIs, files, or browsers (often using the MCP standard). The model requests a tool, the system runs it, and the result comes back.

Memory: Without memory, every turn starts from zero. Short-term memory is the context window. Long-term memory lives in vector stores, files, and knowledge bases. When the window fills up, agents summarize old turns and carry the summary forward.

Loop: All four pieces work together in a cycle. The agent looks at the current state, decides what to do, uses a tool, sees the result, and repeats. It keeps going until it gives a final answer.

Guardrails: Not strictly anatomy, but important. Sandboxing, human checks, token limits, output validation, and scope limits keep autonomy from turning into expensive chaos. The more autonomy you give, the more these matter.

Over to you: when you build an agent, which of these five takes the most work to get right?

You evaluate it. But most teams skip this step (or do it wrong) because “eval” feels vague. It’s not.

Step 1: Pick a task. AI systems have different capabilities and dimensions to evaluate. For LLMs, it can be safety or math capability, in RAGs it can be grounding and retrieval, Pick one.

Step 2: Collect eval data. For every task, gather inputs paired with the right answer or expected behavior. A safety set pairs risky prompts with “refuse.”

Step 3: Develop a grader. How do you decide if the output is good?

Use code-based graders (if/else, unit tests) for things with a clear correct answer and patch passing unit-tests.

Use model-based graders (LLM-as-judge) for subjective tasks like safety.

Use human graders for edge cases and anything where nuance matters more than throughput.

Most production evals combine all three. Code-based for what’s cheap to check. Model-based for scale. Human-based for what matters most.

Over to you: what’s the hardest thing about your task to grade, and which grader type do you use for it?

#35946. Instagram is clearly jealous of YouTube — and is introducing a bunch of changes

Instagram's top executive, Adam Mosseri, has a plan to keep the platform "culturally relevant" — by making it more like YouTube.

Meta-owned Instagram unveiled an overhaul of long-form video features on Monday, including a test of horizontal videos called "Features." Taken together, the changes make the app feel a lot more like YouTube.

"We want to be the most culturally relevant platform," Mosseri said in an interview with content creators and founders of Press Publish, Colin Rosenblum and Samir Chaudry. "To do that, we just need to show up where people are."

The context: YouTube has become a major hit on TV screens, and there's a growing appetite for Instagram-native shows with recurring episodes.

"Subway Takes," hosted by Kareem Rahma, is a prime example of Instagram's strengths and limits. The show started in 2023 as a short-form interview series and, as it grew, expanded with longer-form cuts on YouTube and the streaming platform Tubi. Instagram, however, is where Rahma's show still has its largest audience of 2.4 million followers.

Instagram doesn't want people to go to YouTube or Tubi to see longer versions of shows like "Subway Takes." That's leaving ad revenue and user engagement on the table. Its new tools are designed to entice creators and viewers to stay on its platform.

This isn't Instagram's first time dabbling in long-form content.

In 2018, it launched IGTV, a long-form video format and stand-alone app. In 2020, Instagram rolled out a program that let creators earn money from those videos on IGTV. Four years after launching, the company sunset the IGTV app and shifted its video efforts to Reels.

Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.

There are a few key elements missing from Instagram's big leap (back) into long-form video.

You can't watch the video while continuing to scroll through Instagram, or multitask by opening other apps while keeping the Instagram content in a picture-in-picture pop-up. There also aren't automated transcripts and subtitles yet. A spokesperson for Meta told Business Insider that there are plans for these features.

For creators, one key question and pain point looms: Will Meta share the honeypot?

Mosseri told Rosenblum and Chaudry that Instagram is thinking about ways to test pre-roll and mid-roll ads that could lead to a revenue split. However, he said previous attempts by Instagram to build a creator monetization system comparable to YouTube's Partner Program didn't prove beneficial for the platform.

"We spend more money than we make back by a long shot," Mosseri said of Instagram's previous efforts at broad revenue-sharing programs.

Mosseri said that in its previous iterations of content monetization, creators typically didn't produce more content than they already did for free, and the content they did create tended to be lower quality.

Still, Instagram will need to "figure it out" as it tries to crack longer-form content, Mosseri said.

"I don't think of platform payments or performance-based incentives or bonuses as a revenue driver," Mosseri added. "I'd be psyched to scale them up for any of these formats — as long as we're breaking even."

#35947. Ristorante Sadler a Casa Baglioni Milano: com’è il menu d’autunno

Il ristorante stellato Sadler, al piano rialzato di Casa Baglioni, in via dell’Annunciata a Milano, è una destinazione preferita da una clientela locale e italiana, ma anche per molti stranieri. Che scelgono questa tavola proprio in cerca del ristorante e del suo stile, anche quando alloggiano in altri hotel.

Nella sala molto ampia e luminosa le sedute sono una trentina. C’è anche una wine-cellar che funziona come una saletta-privata.

In sala, ai tavoli, due-tre-coppie e più tardi un tavolo per 8, cena d’affari. Interamente occupata anche la wine-cellar.

Subito evidente, fin dalle prime battute, l’armonia sala-cucina e anche sala-sala. Gentilezza non invasiva. Stile all’altezza della firma prestigiosa. Sbirciamo il menu d’autunno per questa nostra cena stampa.

Il menu della sera prevede piatti alla carta e due percorsi degustazione, quindi intesi per l’intero tavolo.

— Tradizione & Innovazione con 5 portate a 160 euro. — Creativo con 7 portate a 190 euro.

Ogni portata di ciascun percorso è ordinabile anche separatamente a piacere, ma non tutti i prezzi appaiono in carta (verificati in loco il 15 ottobre). Scegliendo alla carta 3 piatti – antipasto, primo e secondo – al ristorante Sadler spendete in media 155 €.

Il menu del Business lunch è più snello e conta 4 antipasti, 4 primi, 4 secondi.

Più una proposta di “Cucina milanese che vive nel tempo e nel futuro”

— Riso al salto (25 €) — Costoletta di vitello alla milanese (38 €)— Piatto unico, di ispirazione valtellinese, con tagliatelle di grano saraceno bietola Casera polpette di vitello e tartufo nero (33 €).

Lo regola non scritta da Sadler è fare la porzione degustazione al 70% del corrispettivo libero. Con pochissime eccezioni, come i tagliolini che, per la loro intensità, sono volutamente in porzione degustazione anche quando richiesti à la carte.

Trovo questo modus operandi trasparente. Specialmente se a confronto con altri percorsi degustazione con piattini non sempre giustificatamente microscopici.

Se fossimo una rivista di moda, diremmo che questo benvenuto è un fashion statement. Diremo invece che dettano la linea, preceduti e accompagnati da una bollicina Ferrari Trentodoc Riserva Lunelli 2016.

— Cannolo di riso al salto, citazione della celebre invenzione da passeggio dello chef Sadler. — Crescenza, crescentina, dragoncello – guscio leggiadro, ultrasottile e quasi podio. Simile nella forma ma diversi nell’esito, l’Airbag-bigné di patate, tartare di manzo (senza manzo nella versione per non carnivori) cipolla rossa, maionese fatta in casa.— Savarin alla pizzaiola, una miniatura da urlo. Primo sul podio.—Tartelletta di impasto udon alla barbabietola, robiola, mirtilli, fiori di sambuco, germogli. Strati sottilissimi e morsi di delicatezza.

E il cestino del pane: Grissini all’olio pericolosi – visto il ripetuto refill, Focaccia barese pomodorini-origano e Shokupan con cipolla rosa dentro impasto, spennellato di miele e cosparso di sale Maldon. Come una nuvola, come di dovere.

Con pagnotta lievito madre arriva l’olio extravergine Trinacria etichettato Sadler, pomodoroso e ancora di medio-alta intensità all’assaggio in purezza.

Siamo due commensali, amiche con gusti diversissimi. Scegliere piatti individuali senza doppioni racconta meglio il menu d’autunno.

Per me, Sashimi di ricciola dello chef Lovato (in carta a 55 €) impiattato a bocciolo, con foglia di shiso, mela verde, cetriolo e completato al tavolo con latticello e olio alla verbena. In una parola, l’oltresashimi. Nel calice Verdemare di Mezzanotte Verdicchio dei Castelli di Jesi 2023, fresco e balsamico.

Dal menu degustazione Tradizione & Innovazione, Capasanta alla plancia con carciofi alla giudia e spuma di dragoncello. Inatteso. Nel calice, Nascetta delle Langhe Castello di Perno. È la seconda volta che bevo questo vitigno, dopo la Trattoria i Bologna a Rocchetta Tanaro. E comunque, piatto gaudioso.

Conclusione trionfale, la Padellata di crostacei (in carta a 110 €) grande classico di Sadler. La mia porzione-degustazione non è affatto piccola, è uno spettacolo. La accompagna una salsina erbacea e titillante. Nel calice, il potente Catarratto Lunarìa di Sergio Genuardi del 2023.

La mia commensale opta per un super autunnale Carpaccio di cervo estratto anche questo dal menu degustazione Tradizione & Innovazione.

Un Kloster Eberbach Crescentia, Riesling Secco Rheingau del 2022 è il pairing perfettamente in grado di reggere la carne rossa.

Seguono i Tagliolini 40 tuorli cotti nel brodo di cipolla e finiti con caviale Asetra, stavolta dal menu degustazione Creativo del ristorante Sadler. Nel calici, Champagne Cossy Cuvée Origine Premier Cru Extra Brut. Intensità.

Il Trancio di rombo alla plancia, salsa di scamorza affumicata, indivia belga, olive taggiasche, acciughe del cantabrico e polvere di capperi (in carta a € 65) è forse il più sobrio dei piatti. Ma solo all’apparenza. Lo completa in pairing un Nebbiolo Sommoclivo Torre San Quirico Azzate.

Al posto del dessert, Orologio di formaggi: Ubriacone affinato nelle vinacce di Amarone, Montebore abba giovane caseificio Guffanti, Castelmagno, Blue Cheese, Gorgonzola. In abbinamento, Champagne, su consiglio della sommelier accettatissimo: Rosélie Rosé de Saignée di Roger Coulon, un Pinot Meunier 100% Vecchie Vigne rosa carico Extra Brut Pas Dosé.

Vermouth Bianco Dorato etichettato Sadler, frutto di una collaborazione chef-sommelier per un commiato dalle sfumature foliage. Si conferma l’amore di Claudio Sadler per le spezie.

Se parliamo tanto di valorizzare la sala, valorizziamola. Ho apprezzato molto vedere al piede del menu di Sadler, i nomi dei singoli professionisti impaginati come se fossero i solisti di un’orchestra sul programma di un concerto.

Giacomo Lovato, executive chef in stretta connessione con Claudio Sadler, ma portatore anche di precedenti esperienze Cracco che vuol dire anche eredità Marchesi.

E i suoi sous-chef con le loro specialità: ladies first, Alice Locatelli, poi Samuele Corti agli antipasti, Jordan Chuquillanqui ai primi, Matteo Sanvito ai secondi. Pamela Pettinari sommelier curiosa e provocatrice. Ci siamo messi felicemente nelle sue mani per i pairing al calice.

Mihai Dragan e Francesca Renda intorno a noi con attenzione, con senso del fluire del servizio e con il sorriso, tutto il tempo. Lo chef Lovato che si è seduto al nostro tavolo a chiacchierare. Quando una persona ama il proprio lavoro, si vede.

#35949. Donald Trump Accused of Rape in Report Linking President to Jeffrey Epstein

President Donald Trump is being accused of historic rape allegations by a former beauty queen as part of a House Judiciary Committee report on the former reality TV star’s alleged ties to the late convicted sex trafficker Jeffrey Epstein.

Rep. Jamie Raskin, the ranking Democratic member of the House Judiciary Committee, released a shocking report on Monday, October 26, that alleges that Trump, 80, was a “co-conspirator” in “Jeffrey Epstein’s conspiracy to traffic and abuse young women in the 1990s,” in addition to being accused by more than 25 women of sexual impropriety throughout his lifetime.

The president has denied ever committing sexual assault. Trump has previously claimed he ended his friendship with Epstein and kicked him out of his Palm Beach resort Mar-A-Lago over allegations the disgraced billionaire “stole” his employees — long before Epstein was convicted of procuring a person under 18 for prostitution and soliciting prostitution in 2008.

Committee staffers spoke with former Swiss beauty queen Beatrice Keul, who previously alleged in a 2024 Daily Mail interview that Trump groped her during an encounter at New York City’s Plaza Hotel in November 1993.

Keul, 55, subsequently told the House committee that she met Trump at the Plaza Hotel in connection with the American Dream Pageant. Once Trump stepped away from their conversation, Epstein — who died by suicide in 2019 after being arrested on fresh sex trafficking charges — allegedly introduced himself to Keul as “Don’s best friend” and offered to arrange her travel to Mar-A-Lago.

The former model further alleges that Trump later raped her in his hotel room, promising to help her arrange a visa and secure her enrollment New York University if she did not go public about the alleged encounter. He later allegedly reiterated his promise when he called her in Switzerland sometime later.

“Upon entering [his hotel room], Trump reportedly grabbed Keul from behind and forcibly raped her; in the process, tearing her dress and causing bleeding and bruising on multiple parts of her body,” the Judiciary Committee report read. “Keul described the rape as ‘brutal.'”

The House report contains direct testimony from four other women who “accused Trump of rape or sexual assault in Epstein’s company or presence.”

“None of these allegations are true. This ‘report’ is nothing more than a desperate attempt by crazed Democrats to distract Americans from the surging Republican momentum driven by President Trump’s historic achievements,” White House spokeswoman Lauren Bis told Us on Monday and as the White House remains without a press secretary. “The President kicked Jeffrey Epstein out of his club at Mar-a-Lago because he was a creep, and he has been fully exonerated of anything related to Epstein.”

Following the publication of the House Judiciary Committee report on Monday, Rep. Raskin, 63, called for the appointment of an independent special counsel to investigate Trump’s alleged ties to Epstein in greater detail.

“Donald Trump says he knew Jeffrey Epstein ‘just like everybody in Palm Beach knew him.’ Not exactly. Evidence involving 11 women tells a completely different and far more damning story about their relationship and the abuse of young women and girls that took place in their network,” Rep. Raskin said in a statement. “Five women have directly accused Trump of rape or sexual assault in Epstein’s company or presence; others have indicated that Epstein or [his accomplice Ghislaine] Maxwell attempted to traffic them to Trump.”

Rep. Raskin alleged that although multiple witnesses and alleged victims have “spoken to investigators and, in some cases, testified under oath,” Trump’s Justice Department has allegedly “shut down investigations, deliberately withheld evidence and, through [Attorney General] Todd Blanche, prevented Ghislaine Maxwell from discussing a survivor whose account implicated Trump — all while insisting there is no basis upon which to investigate Epstein’s possible accomplices.”

“Trump’s Justice Department appears to be covering up these crimes to protect the President,” the committee’s ranking Democrat continued. “The women who survived Epstein deserve to be heard, Congress must have the complete files required by law, and an independent special counsel must investigate Epstein’s co-conspirators and DOJ’s cover-up and ensure that state and foreign prosecutors have the evidence they need to pursue justice.”

In addition to his alleged ties to Epstein, Trump has been accused of sexual impropriety by more than two dozen women.

Writer E. Jean Carroll successfully sued Trump amid accusations of defamation and sexual assault over an alleged incident that occurred in the 1990s in a Bergdorf Goodman department store. Carroll, 82, was awarded $5 million and $83.3 million in damages in two respective judgments. (The Supreme Court denied Trump’s request for the high court to review his appeal of the $5 million judgment in August.)

The 1993 book Lost Tycoon: The Many Lives of Donald Trump included allegations that the mogul committed a “violent assault” against his ex-wife Ivana Trump during their marriage. (Trump called the accusations “obviously false” at the time.)

“[O]n one occasion during 1989, Mr. Trump and I had marital relations in which he behaved very differently toward me than he had during our marriage,” Ivana later said in a statement. “As a woman, I felt violated, as the love and tenderness, which he normally exhibited towards me, was absent. I referred to this as a ‘rape,’ but I do not want my words to be interpreted in a literal or criminal sense.”

Ivana was granted a divorce from Donald in December 1990 on the grounds of “cruel and inhumane” conduct because of his affair with eventual second wife Marla Maples. (Donald and Maples, 62, were married from 1993 to 1999 and he later wed Melania Trump in 2005. Ivana died at age 73 in 2022.)

If you or someone you know has been sexually assaulted, contact the National Sexual Assault Hotline at 1-800-656-HOPE (4673). If you or someone you know is a human trafficking victim, contact the National Human Trafficking Hotline at 1-888-373-7888.

If you or someone you know is struggling or in crisis, help is available. Call or text 988 or chat at 988lifeline.org.

#35950. 冨樫義博氏がXを更新、「HUNTER×HUNTER」第430話の原稿完成を報告

この記事の見出しと要約は、ライブドアニュースが独自の機能で作成したものです。試験提供中の機能のため、記事本文と併せてご確認ください。

『HUNTER×HUNTER』の作者・冨樫義博が10月26日に自身のXを更新し、第430話の原稿完成を報告した。投稿には、連載再開を心待ちにする世界中のファンから歓喜と労いの声が多数寄せられている。

（関連：『HUNTER×HUNTER』が渋谷をジャック！　総勢115キャラが街中に出現「オレが3人分感謝する…」）

『HUNTER×HUNTER』は、第420話「遭遇」をもって一区切りとなっていた。その第420話では、ブラック・ホエール1号の第1層に特殊戒厳令が発令される極限状態のなか、念能力を駆使して自室を抜け出した第4王子・ツェリードニヒがカジノエリアへ潜入。そこで『カイジ』の沼パチンコを思わせる「SWAMP BALL」に興じるヒソカと鉢合わせるという、衝撃的な展開が描かれた。

ヒソカの圧倒的な殺気を前に「刹那の10秒」で間一髪死を回避したツェリードニヒは、底知れぬ恐怖を味わいながらも、ヒソカを「沼男（仮）」と呼び、その強さに歪んだ対抗心を燃やして下層への潜入を図る。今回、第430話まで原稿執筆が進んだことで、ツェリードニヒが巻き起こす波乱のドラマがいよいよ本格化することが期待されている。

読者が最も固唾をのんで見守っているのが、ツェリードニヒとヒソカの再戦の可能性ではないだろうか。ツェリードニヒの特質系能力「刹那の10秒（ラプラスデビル）」は、自らが「絶」を維持している間に限り、10秒先の未来を視て行動を書き換えるというチート級の能力。しかし、百戦錬磨のヒソカに対しては、発動前のわずかな隙や念獣の気配を看破されかけ、あわや一撃で首を落とされるところだった。

常人であれば絶望する実力差を突きつけられながらも、ツェリードニヒは「オレはツェリードニヒ=ホイコーロ!! 諦めの悪い男…！」と、屈辱によって狂気的な成長意欲を滾らせている。天才的なスピードで念を習得してきた怪物が、実戦の死線をくぐったことで予知能力をどう進化させ、再びヒソカの前に立ちはだかるのか。バトル考察の熱は高まるばかりだ。

さらに、ツェリードニヒの単独脱出は、王位継承戦の勢力図そのものを大きく揺るがすことになるだろう。第1層の出入口が封鎖されるなか、彼が目指す第2層以下のフロアでは、血みどろのマフィア抗争が激化している。そこにはヒソカの首を狙う幻影旅団も蠢いており、いつ誰が誰と衝突してもおかしくない一触即発の状況だ。

上位王子の身分を捨て、潜行を試みるツェリードニヒがこの混沌のエリアへ降り立った場合、エイ＝イ一家の無差別殺戮や旅団の動向にどう干渉するのか。王子同士の内毒な駆け引きにとどまらず、船内全域を巻き込んだ群像劇がさらなるカオスへと突入することは間違いない。

そして何より、ファンが息を呑んで待ち望んでいるのが、クラピカとの直接対決だ。ツェリードニヒは、クルタ族の「緋の目」を所有しており、クラピカにとっては同胞の奪還のために必ず対峙しなければならない最大の仇敵である。

これまでツェリードニヒは自室で念の修行に専念しており、第14王子オイトとワブルの護衛に追われるクラピカとは距離を保っていた。しかし、特殊戒厳令をきっかけにツェリードニヒが自室から外へ出たことで、両者の物理的な距離は劇的に縮まった。

幻影旅団以外には使えない「束縛する中指の鎖（チェーンジェイル）」という制約を抱えるクラピカが、未来予知と凶悪な念獣を従えるツェリードニヒをどう迎え撃つのか。寿命を削りながら「絶対時間（エンペラータイム）」を駆使するクラピカの過酷な運命の終着点が、いよいよ現実味を帯びてきている。

#35951. Why State is the Hardest Thing in Software Design

State is the information a system retains that affects its functionality in some way. For example, a website might keep track of users who have logged in. A document editor remembers the current text. A background worker logs which tasks it has completed and which ones are pending. A database stores the records written to it for as long as needed.

Any useful software usually needs to manage state to carry out its intended functionality. The difficulty is keeping the state correct even as requests overlap, servers are added, machines fail, and software changes are deployed.

Often, the common advice is to “make the application stateless”. But this advice needs explanation. It usually means making particular application servers easy to replace while putting their important state elsewhere. It doesn’t signify a complete absence of state. Understanding such a setup requires looking at why state exists, who owns it, and what happens when it becomes unavailable.

In this article, we will look at why state is the hardest thing in software development and the multiple strategies that developers can use to manage it.

#35953. National Electoral Institute president resigns as corruption questions mount

Amid allegations of corruption, the president of the National Electoral Institute (INE), Guadalupe Taddei, announced her resignation on Sunday.

“After deep reflection, I have decided to present my resignation tomorrow, Oct. 26, to the Chamber of Deputies of the Congress of the Union,” Taddei said in a statement.

#BoletínINE 📄 | Mensaje de la Consejera Presidenta del Instituto Nacional Electoral, Guadalupe Taddei Zavala.

👇 Los detalles de la información en el enlace.

🔗 https://t.co/KZDmM4qKfV https://t.co/syPTgTYKPn

— @INEMexico (@INEMexico) October 25, 2026

“… This decision reflects my conviction that protecting the [National Electoral] Institute and allowing it to continue fulfilling its constitutional mission, must always take precedence over any temporary or personal interest,” she said.

It is the first time since the Federal Electoral Institute (the INE’s predecessor) was founded in 1990 that a president of the electoral authority has resigned. Luis Carlos Ugalde, a former chief of the electoral authority, told Bloomberg Línea that both allegations of corruption against an electoral chief and Taddei’s resignation as INE president are “unprecedented.”

Taddei’s announcement came after Mexican media outlets, including Reforma, El Universal and Latinus, reported last week on alleged irregularities in INE contracts and her ties to businessman José Alfredo Hernández Casillas. The outgoing INE president reportedly has a close personal relationship with Hernández, whose family’s companies have received lucrative INE contracts.

In an article published on Monday under the headline “Taddei is leaving,” the newspaper Reforma noted that it documented how Hernández — who is linked to various electoral materials suppliers — “obtained [electoral authority] contracts since Taddei presided over the electoral organization of Sonora” between 2014 and 2021.

Taddei’s “closeness” to Hernández allowed the latter to obtain contracts awarded by high-ranking INE officials, Reforma reported. The newspaper also reported that Jonathan Navarrete Torres, an employee of Hernández, was on the INE’s payroll as Taddei’s chauffeur without actually working in that role.

In addition, Reforma reported on Monday that it had “revealed” that seven INE suppliers remained under investigation by the federal government’s Financial Intelligence Unit for money laundering and “simulation” in bidding processes. The allegation is that seven companies linked to Hernández and his brother feigned competition for contracts awarded by the INE and the State Electoral Institute in Sonora under Taddei’s leadership.

Reforma also noted that video footage had emerged showing Taddei and two of her children socializing with Hernández.

“One video filmed by the businessman shows a son of Taddei [Jorge Francisco Piñeda Taddei] enjoying a stay at the most expensive hotel in Paris,” Reforma reported. “‘Paris at your feet, young man,’ the businessman says in the video, boasting about the gift.”

In light of the various media reports about alleged corruption at the INE, the Federal Auditor’s Office (ASF) announced on Friday that it had begun an investigation into allegations regarding the “improper use of public resources.”

In a statement, the ASF said the investigation “covers” the INE, state-based electoral authorities, companies, public servants and suppliers “that are related to each other.”

The ASF said there is “already evidence … related to irregularities in several contracts” as well as conflicts of interest, collusion and triangulation of resources.”

It said it would investigate allegations involving the INE with “complete independence, objectivity and technical rigor.”

“A Mexico without corruption is possible,” concluded the ASF statement.

In her statement on Sunday, Taddei acknowledged the accusations that have been leveled against her.

“I am aware of the allegations that have circulated in the media and on social media in recent days, including rumors, fabrications, leaks and accounts whose authenticity, context and purpose — as in all cases — must be verified and corroborated,” she said.

“… Each allegation must be examined with evidence and through institutional channels,” Taddei said.

In a separate statement on Saturday, she said that “no hiring process, public servant or supplier is above the law.”

Taddei also said that some high-ranking INE officials had been stood down, but she didn’t name them.

The outgoing INE president said on Sunday she was resigning “with calmness and dignity” after dedicating 33 years of her life “to electoral service in Mexico.”

Taddei said her decision “does not relieve anyone of the duty to clarify, with transparency, rigor and due process, the events that must be investigated.”

The 63-year-old Sonora native began what was supposed to be a nine-year term at the INE in early 2023. Mexico News Daily reported at the time that Taddei had family links to both the federal government and the ruling Morena party.

The opposition National Action Party (PAN) said at the time that it was “completely unacceptable that the person who seeks to preside over our electoral umpire is clearly linked to Morena.”

The PAN had called for Taddei’s resignation in light of the corruption allegations outlined in various media reports.

“If contracts were rigged, we are very concerned that elections will be rigged as well,” said PAN national president Jorge Romero.

At her Monday morning press conference, President Claudia Sheinbaum described Taddei’s decision to step down as INE president as “good.”

“… Obviously, an investigation has to be carried out. … There has to be an audit in order to review the suspicions or the allegations there have been regarding abuses or privileges within the INE,” she said.

Sheinbaum stressed that there will be no interruption to the INE’s work despite Taddei’s decision to resign. One of the INE’s electoral councilors will head up the electoral authority until the Chamber of Deputies appoints a new president.

The INE’s main focus is currently the federal, state and municipal elections that will be held on June 6, 2027.

With reports from EFE, Reforma and Bloomberg Línea

#35955. Matteo Baronetto ha trovato casa per il suo ristorante: in una serra

Sono trascorsi quasi due anni da quando Matteo Baronetto ha lasciato la guida della cucina del ristorante stellato Del Cambio a Torino. E ci si chiedeva quale sarebbe stato il progetto personale di cui si parlava nella nota ufficiale. Intanto è nel team del festival gastronomico Buonissima che prevede oltre 200 appuntamenti diffusi in città. Ed è qui che cercavamo conferma del rumor di questi giorni: Matteo Baronetto aprirà il suo nuovo (quasi) ristorante in una serra.

La serra è quella del parco di Villa Sanquirico a Torino in via Federico Campana 37 di fronte al Parco del Valentino. Dove si sta lavorando per creare una cucina a vista con un unico chef table per 6/8 posti.

Per Buonissima, nella dimora tardo-ottocentesca torinese, venerdì 23 ottobre dalle ore 20, si terrà “l’esclusiva cena a cura di Dino Como di Sextantio Cucina a Santo Stefano di Sessanio (AQ)”. All’interno dell’edificio, però.

I lavori, invece, interessano appunto la serra e hanno già dotato questo spazio di un ingresso dedicato che porta direttamente al tavolo e alla cucina di Matteo Baronetto. Quindi, se avete prenotato la cena di Buonissima del 23 ottobre, gettate un occhio per confermare l’indiscrezione.

Una nota ufficiale ancora non è stata diramata e vedremo se il ristorante di cui si vocifera aprirà a ridosso delle festività natalizie o al più tardi con il primo sole primaverile.

Matteo Baronetto con questo ristorante pensa a pranzi e cene tagliate su misura degli ospiti e solo su prenotazione. Il repertorio dei piatti dello chef allievo di Gualtiero Marchesi e sous chef di Carlo Cracco a Milano è ampio. E non mancano le ricette della cucina tradizionale rivisitata in chiave contemporanea. Come ci ricordano le 45 ricette pubblicate nel suo libro Cucina Contemporanea Piemontese uscito per i tipi di EDT con le foto di Davide Dutto.

L’insalata russa, la bagna cauda, il fritto misto, i tajarin e gli agnolotti, il gran bollito e il bonet potrebbero trovare casa in un’ambientazione tradizionale come la serra di Villa Sanquirico, ma con il tocco contemporaneo di Baronetto. Che spesso si è dichiarato custode della storia culinaria piemontese. Almeno fino a quando era al Del Cambio.

Per il futuro della Serra Baronetto si vedrà tra qualche mese.

#35958. Billy Evans suggested that full-frontal shower scene in 'You Can See Everything,' the director says

In "You Can See Everything," one person apparently took the movie's title to a physical extreme.

A24's twisty new documentary is ostensibly focused on Theranos founder Elizabeth Holmes, one of Silicon Valley's most infamous felons — yet it's her longtime partner, William "Billy" Evans, who frequently steals the show.

Evans' comfort in front of the camera is evident when the couple invites co-directors Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim to capture intimate moments in their home life, just a few weeks before Holmes is scheduled to begin an 11-year prison sentence.

In fact, according to Oppenheim, Evans proactively suggested one of the movie's buzziest and most revealing moments: a full-frontal shot of Evans in the shower.

"We were in the car ride on the way back from the gym, and I said to Billy, 'We will give you some time to shower,'" the filmmaker said in a recent interview with GQ. "And Billy responded, 'Want to film me showering?' I offered to film him in a way that could avoid seeing his genitals, but Billy said he didn't care about being seen naked."

Oppenheim speculated that Evans was inspired to give the audience "full access," though he admitted that he was personally baffled by the choice.

"There's a camera in the bathroom, there's a camera that was on Nathan, there's a camera outside the bathroom, and there's an interaction that we see after Billy leaves where he sort of looks at the camera and smiles," Oppenheim said. "So I don't know what that means, but I feel like there's enough stuff in there that gives me the impression that maybe he was excited that people could see everything and that there was full access, I guess. But it really beats me, honestly."

Oppenheim said that, to his knowledge, neither Holmes nor Evans has seen the movie. Evans has not commented publicly on his portrayal, and he did not respond to a request for comment from Business Insider.

"We haven't talked with Billy in a bit now," Oppenheim told GQ. "You sort of see it in the film. Billy can be intense, and there were a lot of conversations he had about the future of the film and the release of the film that made us uncomfortable. So contact broke down for a bit."

#35960. How DoorDash Built a Toolbox for AI Agents

CodeAF is a new open-source software factory that sits on the Pareto frontier of cost, speed and quality. On DeepSWE it solved nearly 4× as many real GitHub issues as Claude Code on the same open model, and matched the official leaderboard result at half the cost.

It is built from the ground up for the next era of coding, where you stop chatting with agents and start directing them. Built for open models like DeepSeek, Qwen, GLM and Kimi, it gives you frontier-grade coding without locking you into a closed model, and it still works with any provider you choose.

The utility of AI agents increases when they can take real actions in real systems. This involves the use of tools. While MCP made it easier to expose these tools, there were a lot of other concerns that had to be handled in order to make it work at an enterprise level.

DoorDash built a shared Agent Gateway to control how AI agents discover and use tools. The gateway brings together several responsibilities: checking permissions, managing credentials, choosing which tools an agent can see, forwarding requests, and recording what happened.

In this article, we will look at how the DoorDash engineering team built this gateway and the decisions they made. Here’s what we will cover:

Verifying who is calling and what they can do

Why identifying the caller is different from supplying credentials

How a user connects an account during a tool call

What happens during discovery and execution

How DoorDash made the platform easy to adopt

Disclaimer: This post is based on publicly shared details from various sources. References at the end. Please comment if you notice any inaccuracies.

A language model can write a super-detailed explanation of how to investigate a software problem, but it doesn’t automatically have access to a company’s code repositories, incident reports, or production logs. We need to provide access to those systems through software. From the perspective of the model, these systems are like tools.

An AI agent is an application that uses a language model to decide which steps and tools to use while working on a particular task. A tool is a specific capability that the application makes available to the model. For example, searching documentation, reading a support ticket, and opening a pull request.

When the model selects a tool, the surrounding application uses the tool to execute the required operation and supplies the result back to the language model. The model can then use that result to decide what to do next. For example, an agent investigating a failed software build might retrieve the build logs, inspect relevant code, and use what it finds to explain the failure.

Some tools only read information. Others can also change something in a real system, such as updating a ticket or creating a pull request. This makes tool access a very important part of an agent’s design. It determines what the agent can learn and what it can eventually do with that learning. At DoorDash, these capabilities come from many places, including internal services, engineering systems, documentation platforms, and third-party software.

Different systems or tools normally expose different interfaces. An API is a way for one program to request information or actions from another program. Without a shared approach, an AI agent application has to accommodate the particular interfaces of every tool it uses.

The Model Context Protocol (MCP) provides a common way to describe, discover, and invoke capabilities. An MCP server exposes tools, and an MCP client communicates with that server. The agent application uses an MCP client to access the tools.

This is largely facilitated by two main operations:

The first is tools/list, which discovers the tools available from a server. The returned catalog gives the agent information about the available capabilities, including their names, descriptions, and expected inputs.

The second is tools/call, which requests execution of a particular tool with the supplied inputs.

For example, a server might expose a tool for looking up an issue in the issue tracker. A discovery service tells the agent that this capability exists and how to request it. Invocation asks the server to perform a specific lookup.

Such a shared interface makes integration simple. However, DoorDash still had to decide which agents can use which tools, whose account an action should use, and how access should be monitored. A coding agent might need access to GitHub, Jira, code search, documentation, and systems that show production behavior. Each connection introduces decisions about permissions, credentials, available operations, and monitoring.

This makes the problem of tool access not just a matter of connection. Also, there are a bunch of company-specific rules that come into the picture here. MCP’s common interface doesn’t establish those company-specific rules. If every team implements these responsibilities independently, the same work gets repeated across many agents and servers. For example, one team might build an OAuth connection flow, another might develop its own secret handling, and another creates a different way to record tool usage. It becomes quite difficult to maintain consistent behavior.

DoorDash organizes this problem into three separate concerns:

Access: Deals with the identity and permissions behind a request. The platform needs to know who is calling, what that caller is allowed to do, and which credentials the downstream system requires. For example, an agent acting on behalf of an individual employee may need a different credential arrangement from an automated process running for an entire team.

Tool-surface Curation: This concern handles the capabilities presented to the agent. A downstream server might offer hundreds of tools, while a particular workflow only needs a small subset. DoorDash needed a way to select the relevant, approved tools instead of exposing the entire catalog.

Operations: This concerns what happens when these integrations run in production. Teams need to understand traffic, failures, response times, usage, and costs. They also need controls that prevent excessive requests from overwhelming downstream systems.

The Agent Gateway brings all of these concerns and responsibilities into a shared platform. Let’s look at that in more detail.

A gateway is a service through which requests pass before reaching their destination.

In DoorDash’s design, an agent sends its MCP requests to the Agent Gateway, which controls access and forwards approved requests to the appropriate downstream MCP server. Here, downstream simply means the system that receives a request after the gateway forwards it.

There are two core components involved here: a proxy and a registry.

The proxy handles requests as they arrive. It verifies the caller, checks permissions, applies rate limits, attaches the required credentials, and forwards the request. It also produces information used for monitoring and auditing. Since it handles the actual request traffic, it is considered part of the data plane.

The registry stores the information needed to govern the incoming traffic. This includes details about the registered agents and MCP servers, ownership details, connection settings, authentication modes, policies, discovered tool catalogs, and configurations that determine which tools are exposed.

The registry is the source of truth for the control plane, which manages how the system should operate. The engineering teams use a management interface and API to configure the gateway, while the proxy applies the resulting configuration to requests.

This division between the two components separates the work of configuring access from the work of handling traffic. A tool owner can register a capability and attach policy through the control plane. The proxy then enforces that policy when the AI agents need to use the capability.

DoorDash also separates internal workflows and external-facing use cases into different proxy planes. They share libraries and registry concepts. But they maintain separate trust boundaries. Think of it as a common governance approach without implying that every request passes through one physical proxy instance

Two closely related concepts play a key role in the Agent Gateway built by the DoorDash engineering team. These concepts are authentication and authorization.

Authentication establishes the identity of the caller making the request. A request might come from a user, a software service, or an agent acting on behalf of a user. Authorization determines what that identified caller is allowed to do.

These checks need more information than simply deciding whether someone can access an entire MCP server. A server may expose both harmless lookup operations and powerful administrative actions. The distinction between the two should be clear. For example, permission to search a repository should not automatically imply permission to use every operation the server provides.

DoorDash’s agent gateway can evaluate permissions involving several pieces of context. It can consider the agent, the user, the requested tool, and the environment in which the request occurs. It can also distinguish access to read-only capabilities from access to capabilities that modify data. For example, a policy might allow an agent to read issue details on behalf of a user while preventing that agent from performing administrative operations. Another workflow might be allowed to run under a team identity, while a user-specific action requires the individual user’s authorization.

The agent gateway carries the identity of the caller through the rest of the request process, including routing and recording usage. That makes it possible to attribute a downstream action to the context that caused it.

Centralizing these decisions also gives DoorDash a shared place to change or revoke access if needed. Agent developers don’t have to encode security decisions in prompts. Also, each tool owner doesn’t have to rebuild the same gateway-level authorization logic.

Even after the agent gateway knows who is making a request and whether it is allowed, the downstream system may require its own credentials.

A credential is something used to establish identity or access, such as an API key or an access token. The identity used to enter DoorDash’s gateway and the credential required by a third-party service are separate parts of the request.

For instance, the gateway may recognize an employee through the internal identity system. However, a documentation provider may still require that employee’s authorization token before it allows access to their documents. There are four types of credential arrangements that are possible:

Internal Service Identity: This is an identity verified by the DoorDash infrastructure. It forwards verified caller context to internal services.

Gateway-held Token: This is a vendor or service token kept in gateway secret storage. It adds the token to the downstream request without giving it to the agent.

Per-user OAuth: This is for access that a particular user has granted to an external service. The gateway stores the grant securely, supplies the user’s token, and refreshes it when needed.

Service Principal: This is a non-personal identity used by software or team automation. The gateway obtains or issues short-lived credentials through a gateway-managed team identity.

Credential injection means adding the appropriate credential to the outgoing request before forwarding it. The agent asks to use a tool. The gateway handles the credential needed to access the system behind that tool.

The design created by the DoorDash engineering team keeps raw downstream credentials, including vendor keys and OAuth refresh tokens, out of the agents. This gives the platform a central place to manage, rotate, audit, and revoke those credentials.

Some tools need to act within a particular user’s account. For example, reading that user’s documents or updating a ticket as that user requires appropriate user authorization.

OAuth is a mechanism through which a user authorizes an application to access a service with specified permissions. The application receives tokens that it can use for the authorized access.

The access token is used when making requests. A refresh token, when issued, allows the application to obtain a replacement access token when the current one expires.

Before the agent gateway, the product teams within DoorDash tended to build their own connection flows and token storage. However, the gateway centralizes this work.

When a call needs authorization that is not yet available, the gateway starts the provider’s OAuth flow. After the user authorizes access, the gateway stores the resulting tokens in encrypted form and supplies the appropriate token on future calls.

For clients that support MCP elicitation, the gateway can ask the client to present a connection prompt while keeping the original tool request open. The user completes authorization in a browser. The gateway then obtains and stores the token, resumes the original request with that token, and returns the result from the tool.

To clarify things here, elicitation is the protocol mechanism used to request the user interaction needed to continue the task. The user completes the provider’s authorization flow. However, the agent doesn’t receive the raw credential.

For clients without elicitation support, the gateway returns a structured response explaining that authorization is required, along with a connection URL. This gives the client a clear way to recover, even though it cannot use the same pause-and-resume interaction.

Permissions determine which actions are allowed, but the tool surface shown to an agent also affects its behavior.

Tool surface means the set of tools presented to an agent, including their names, descriptions, and organization. A server’s complete catalog may contain administrative functions, billing operations, destructive actions, and features unrelated to the agent’s task. Presenting all of those capabilities creates unnecessary choices. The model has more descriptions to interpret and more potentially similar tools to distinguish. The approach used by the DoorDash engineering team is to expose smaller, relevant catalogs that match the work being performed.

There are two mechanisms behind this: bundles and filters.

A bundle combines tools from multiple MCP servers behind one logical MCP endpoint. An endpoint is the address to which the client sends requests. For example, a developer-tools bundle can provide selected GitHub, Jira, observability, code-search, and documentation capabilities through one gateway address. The tools still run through their respective downstream servers. However, the bundle gives the agent one organized interface through which to discover and use them.

Filters determine which tools appear in that interface. Their decisions can depend on the bundle, agent, user group, environment, or intended audience. A provider might expose both issue lookup and administrative operations, while a particular bundle includes only the approved issue-related capabilities.

The agent gateway can also give tools stable names and clearer descriptions. When names from different servers overlap, namespacing or aliases can help distinguish them. Namespacing means adding identifying context to a name, such as identifying the service to which a tool belongs.

Discovery and execution are related, but they happen at different points and require their own checks and validations.

During discovery, the agent sends tools/list to a bundle endpoint. The gateway gathers tool information from the servers included in that bundle. This is known as fan-out, meaning that one incoming request leads to requests across multiple downstream servers.

The gateway then applies authorization and filtering rules. It combines the approved tools into a coherent catalog, adjusts names where necessary, and returns the result to the agent. The agent might therefore receive a catalog containing repository tools from one server, issue tools from another, and log-query tools from a third. It interacts with the gateway’s combined interface instead of managing each server’s setup separately.

When the agent chooses a tool, it sends a tools/call request to the gateway. The gateway checks policy again, applies the relevant rate limits, determines the correct downstream server, and supplies the required credential. It then forwards the request and returns the result.

A tool appearing in a discovery response doesn’t remove the need to authorize its execution.

In other words, discovery controls what is presented to the agent. Invocation controls whether a particular request is allowed to proceed.

This gives the gateway responsibility for both the available catalog and the actual use of its tools. It also means the agent doesn’t need to implement downstream routing. Neither does it have to select credentials for each provider.

Once many agents have used many tools, the teams need to understand more about the resulting activity. This comes under the area of observability, which means the ability to understand a running system through the information it produces.

The gateway acts as a useful component for this because all tool requests pass through it. It can record a structured event for each call, with consistent fields describing the server, tool, bundle, owner, and identities involved. For reference, a structured event is a record with defined fields that can be searched or aggregated. Along with identity and ownership, an event can also contain the authorization result, request status, error source, timing information, and request and response sizes.

This helps answer questions such as which agent generated a request, which tool failed, and which team owns the affected capability.

The gateway also produces metrics, which summarize behavior across requests. This includes request counts, tool response times, authorization decisions, OAuth refresh outcomes, rate-limit decisions, and downstream failures.

These different forms of information serve different groups within the enterprise. Security teams can audit access, platform teams can investigate unusually active agents, and tool owners can understand adoption and failures.

A centralized gateway is useful only if teams can use it without excessive effort. Therefore, DoorDash makes registration and configuration for the gateway available through a self-service management interface and API.

A team starts by registering its MCP server. The platform discovers the server’s raw catalog through tools/list, after which the tools intended for exposure are selected and approved.

The team attaches the authentication mode, ownership information, and access policy.

Approved tools are then added to one or more bundles. Once agents begin using them, teams can inspect traffic, response times, failures, authorization decisions, and cost information.

As we can see, registration is the beginning of onboarding. Discovering that a server offers a capability doesn’t automatically mean that every agent receives access to it. Ownership information is also part of making the system operationally useful. When a capability fails or needs a policy change, the platform needs a clear association between that capability and the team responsible for it.

The gateway has now become the default path for agent-tool access across DoorDash engineering teams. DoorDash has reported more than 200 registered MCP servers, more than 30 agents and services used by thousands of employees, and millions of tool calls each week.

DoorDash is making further investments in the gateway.

One planned improvement is stronger agent identity and user delegation. Delegation means allowing an agent to perform an action on someone else’s behalf within defined permissions.

DoorDash’s target model gives each agent a cryptographic identity and uses short-lived delegated credentials scoped to the user, agent, task, and target tool. This would make it possible to determine the connection between the user requesting work, the agent performing it, and the specific action being authorized.

Another planned improvement is dynamic tool discovery. Existing bundles already narrow the tools available to an agent. Dynamic discovery would narrow that selection further using the current task, access policy, and usage signals, so the agent receives the capabilities most likely to help with its present work.

There are also plans to improve evaluation of tool quality and security, identifying risky descriptions, detecting secrets or personally identifiable information in errors, simplifying server creation and registration, and producing redacted tool-call event streams.

These investments extend the same architectural approach. As agents gain access to more systems, the platform needs precise ways to establish who is acting, expose useful capabilities, authorize specific actions, and make the resulting activity understandable.

How DoorDash Built a Centralized Gateway for AI Agent-Tool Access

Honestly the least glamorous part of the agent story is the one that actually matters: who's allowed to do what, with whose credentials, and a log of what happened afterwards. Everyone wants to demo the agent opening the pull request. Nobody wants to build the room where you check whether it was allowed to.

The split between caller identity and downstream credentials is the part I wish more agent tutorials started with. When something acts on a user's behalf, collapsing those two layers makes failures hard to debug: you think the agent was allowed to act, but the outbound call used the wrong account. For anything that sends or mutates user data, I treat gateway-held and per-user tokens as different trust zones, and the agent never sees either. How are teams deciding which tools get per-user OAuth versus a shared service principal?

#35963. Jason Oppenheim Addresses Ex Chrishell Stause’s Split From Partner G Flip

Jason Oppenheim is ready to be a shoulder for ex Chrishell Stause following her shocking split from G Flip.

Oppenheim, 49, was quick to support Stause, 45, on Monday, October 26, after she announced her and G Flip’s breakup after three years of marriage and a very public fertility journey.

“I’m really sorry to hear this,” he wrote in the comments section of Stause’s emotional video announcement shared via Instagram. “I’m thinking of you and sending you my love.”

Stause revealed hours earlier that she and the Australian singer, 33, were “no longer together,” admitting she feels “like a little bit of a failure” after her second broken marriage. (Stause was previously married to Justin Hartley. The pair split in 2019 after two years of marriage, finalizing the divorce in 2021.)

Oppenheim, however, reassured his former Selling Sunset costar that she was not a failure.

“You’re both beautiful people and sometimes things just don’t work out, but it certainly doesn’t mean anyone failed,” he wrote. “There’s probably nothing anyone can say to make you feel better right now, it might just take some time to process.”

Oppenheim told Stause, “Please know that you’re very loved and I’m always there for you no matter what. ❤️.”

Following Stause’s divorce from Hartley, 49, she briefly dated Jason while working for him at the Oppenheim Group, which he founded with his twin brother, Brett Oppenheim.

The pair sparked romance speculation in July 2021 while on a cast trip to Italy, where Stause was spotted getting cozy with Jason.

“Chrishell and I became close friends and it has developed into an amazing relationship,” Jason exclusively told Us that same month. “I care about her deeply and we’re very happy together.”

Stause and Jason’s whirlwind romance, however, came to an end before the end of the year.

“While Chrishell and I are no longer together, we remain best friends. We will always love and support one another,” Jason confirmed via Instagram in December 2021. “She was the most amazing girlfriend I’ve ever had, and it was the happiest and most fulfilling relationship of my life.

Stause moved on with G Flip in 2022 and the couple got married in May 2023.

Throughout their relationship, Stause was open about her infertility struggles as she and G Flip attempted to start a family.

Less than one year after Stause told Bustle in November 2025 that she was considering giving IVF “one more chance” after her Selling Sunset exit, she and G Flip called it quits.

“We have agreed to treat this public separation with love and respect for each other. That’s not hard for me because I am in love with this person,” Stause said in her Instagram video on Monday. “I don’t have all the information or the reasons. I’m sure just as always with time you get the lessons you’re meant to get.”

G Flip broke their silence on the split that same afternoon, writing in their own social media statement, “After 4+ years Chrishell and I are no longer together. I will always love her, we will always share a special connection and I cherish our memories together deeply.”

The “Bed on Fire” singer added, “My head & heart are feeling very heavy right now and I’m not able to talk but I want to thank you all for your support and appreciate you respecting our privacy at this difficult time.”

#35966. Mexico sends ultrarunners and a photo exhibit to El Salvador to promote Indigenous sporting culture

A delegation of high-performance Mexican athletes will travel to El Salvador to participate in next month’s Cordillera Challenge, a 130-kilometer mountain race with over 6,300 meters of positive elevation gain.

The participation of the four runners is part of a government-sponsored agenda seeking to strengthen sporting and cultural ties between both countries.

María Lorena Ramírez Hernández, an Indigenous ultramarathon runner originally from the Sierra Tarahumara in the state of Chihuahua, will lead the delegation.

The 31-year-old Ramírez, a 2025 National Sports Award recipient, gained international recognition after winning the UltraTrail Cerro Rojo in 2017, an ultra-distance race of 80 kilometers (50 miles), while wearing huaraches, a type of traditional sandal. She finished with a time of 7 hours, 20 minutes.

The Cordillera Challenge — a 130-kilometer, multi-day endurance race taking place from Nov. 6-8 — will traverse some of El Salvador’s most stunning natural landscapes, including Lago de Coatepeque (a 115-meter-deep crater lake), the Izalco and Ataco volcanoes and the coastal sandbar known as Barra de Santiago.

Mirna Beatriz de la Cruz, leader of México Imparable, a government initiative that links sport and cultural identity to promote athletic talent and give visibility to Mexico’s Indigenous peoples, will also attend the 3-day event.

Upon establishing México Imparable in August 2025, President Claudia Sheinbaum described the initiative as “a symbolic action that represents the strength and resilience of all the Mexican people, particularly the indigenous communities.”

México Imparable’s agenda in El Salvador includes the photo exhibition “Mexico, Land of Champions,” which will be inaugurated on Nov. 3 at the National Library in San Salvador.

The exhibition — sponsored by the Mexican Embassy — includes images of daily life, traditions and the rich cultural heritage of the Sierra Tarahumara’s Indigenous communities.

Through portraits of its inhabitants, their customs, their relationship with the land and their connection to sport, the exhibit seeks to introduce the Salvadoran public to the cultural diversity of the Rarámuri and highlight their contribution to Mexico’s heritage.

#35967. 335 Chiocciole Slow Food, cioè le migliori osterie 2027: eccole

È uscita la guida Osterie d’Italia 2027 di Slow Food che censisce 1952 indirizzi in Italia e giudica come migliori osterie 335 locali che ricevono la Chiocciola.

Di questa corposa selezione, fanno parte 175 Locali Quotidiani, ovvero i pastifici, le gastronomie, le enoteche con cucina, che per la prima volta compaiono come sezioni regionali e non più raggruppati in fondo al volume.

In più, come ogni anno, la guida ospita gli inserti regionali con i bacari veneziani, i buffet triestini, le malghe trentine, i fornelli pugliesi, i trippai fiorentini e tanti altri locali tipici di ogni regione.

My professional background has given me extensive experience with psychiatric diagnoses, symptoms and medications, and this is an area I take very seriously.”

Conrad continued, “When Collin came to live with us, I did not observe the symptoms or behaviors I would have expected based on some of the diagnoses he had received, nor did I observe the behaviors that have since been publicly described. In consultation with his treating physician, we gradually discontinued his medications under medical supervision.”

Jon’s ex reminded her followers that mental health conditions are “real, complex and deserving of compassion and understanding.”

“However, they should never be used to dismiss another person’s lived experience or deflect legitimate questions about the past,” she continued. “I believe these issues deserve to be addressed with honesty, responsibility and respect for the people involved.”

These allegations are categorically false.”

#35969. I ran Anthropic's 335-page IPO filing through an AI-writing detector. Well, well, well…

Senior Correspondent covering technology and culture

Anthropic's much-awaited S-1 filing landed today, revealing all sorts of interesting things about the company's plans and current finances.

But the biggest question I had wasn't "does Anthropic think that accidentally destroying humanity is a business risk?" (yes) or "how much money are they losing?" (lots) or "does it feel weird to be part of the 50% of employees who don't do the pledge to donate some of their equity for the greater good?" (still wondering — if this is you, let me know).

My question was: Did they use AI to write this thing?

To find out, I ran the whole 335-page S-1 filing through Pangram, a service that checks for signs of AI-writing. Ok, well technically I tried to run it through myself, but ran into some problems with such a massive file. I reached out to Pangram CEO and cofounder Max Spero, and he ran the full file for me.

(I also reached out to Anthropic for comment about the tool's results. I haven't heard back — I suspect it's a busy day for them.)

And before you ask me why I didn't use Anthropic's official AI watermarking system to check, it's not available to normies yet.

Here's what Pangram's result was for the full 259,031 words on the S-1 filing:

So there was a tiny bit of AI use, per Pangram. It flagged some sentences and passages it said with a "high" degree of confidence contained AI use, things like: "The pace of AI development is extremely rapid, with successive versions of models emerging in compressed timeframes, often measured in months rather than years."

Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.

"My read on a result like this is that it indicates light AI editing or AI usage at the sentence level," Spero told me. "It does not seem like any significant sections of the document are fully AI-generated."

This is one of the limitations of something like Pangram, which isn't 100% perfect, and the way that people use AI tools these days for editing and proofreading might affect results.

My colleague Aki Ito ran all her LinkedIn posts and published articles here on Business Insider through Pangram as an experiment, and ended up with a tiny surprise: one sentence in one article was flagged as AI-written. When she looked back at it, she saw that ChatGPT had suggested she rephrase her original, human-written sentence, and she accepted the new wording. (I use Grammarly, an AI-powered grammar tool that mainly helps with my repeated crimes against commas, but occasionally suggests rewording, which I sometimes accept and sometimes reject, though I worry that this may get flagged as AI).

So it would appear Anthropic still had real humans — slow, expensive, fallible meatsacks — write this massive document, with just a little assist from Claude in the editing. That might be par for the course these days for IPO teams and S-1's, or at the very least the S-1's for literal AI companies. (If you're a banker who works on S-1s, shoot me an email!)

I can't tell if I think this is a good sign or a bad sign for Anthropic (and the world)?

On one hand, shouldn't Anthropic be eating its own dog food, so to speak, and proudly use its own AI for something like this? An S-1 filing is full of the kind of boilerplate, charmless, technical-ish writing that you'd assume AI would be perfect to replace a human for. If Anthropic is going to the hassle of having real (presumably highly paid humans) write out each of the 250,000+ words here, well, where is its faith in its own product? I you can't trust Claude to write your S-1, can you trust it to cure cancer?

On the other hand, thank god this isn't 100% AI-written. The stakes are pretty high when the SEC is poring over every word. If you're going to tell people your company is worth hundreds of billions of dollars, at least have a human show up and type it out, eh?

I guess bankers everywhere can breathe a sigh of relief.

#35970. La trattoria stellare da assoldare per il vostro matrimonio è a Eboli

Il nome lo conosce bene, benissimo, chi è in procinto di organizzare il proprio matrimonio (o è invitato): Trotta&Trotta Quality Banqueting. Siamo ad Eboli e il secondo Trotta della “&” è Pasquale “Paky”. Che si è messo in testa una bellissima idea: affiancare al core business del wedding un ristorante. Invero particolare, Somnium, di cui avete già letto su queste pagine. E su cui molti preconizzano l’arrivo di un astro brillante. Forgiato tra ennemila banchetti e un curriculum da tristellati (uno per tutti, Heinz Beck), ha avviato un calendario di appuntamenti che si incastrano nelle rade aperture e nei pochi posti a disposizione per i commensali. 16 scranni fronte cucina e orari di apertura variabili come nei calendari nordici (4 su 7 e non sempre di sabato). Un solo menu al buio in ambiente distopico. I 4 mani con pizzaioli famosi e colleghi. E poi la trattoria da matrimonio, distopici entrambi.

Il nome non ufficiale è Trattoria del 3000 e si propone come Trottaria, la trattoria di Paky Trotta per l’appunto.

Per ora 2 appuntamenti a circuito chiuso con il prossimo in programma il 21 ottobre (prenotazioni al 347 781 0834).

Prezzo 100 euro, escluso vino — da scegliere al bancone della selezione.

Trattasi dunque di evento speciale il cui menu varia al variare dell’umore del “trottore” e dei commensali che prendono parte alla tavolata. Per l’occasione spostata in 28 posti direttamente al passi della cucina. O nel salottino dell’aperitivo dominato dalle inquietanti scene distopiche del maxi schermo.

Ha funzionato? Sì. Andamento divertente con classici rivisitati. Niente tortelli ripieni di astice, il piatto per eccellenza dei catering matrimoniali. L’astice fa effetto e segna il livello di importanza della celebrazione. Il tortello racchiude e si cuoce a tempo record per respingere gli assalti delle legioni affamate dall’inevitabile caldo amplificato dalle mise d’ordinanza.

E cosa si è mangiato nella Trattoria del 3000 il cui menu dovrebbe finire diritto diritto nelle ville panoramiche dei matrimoni?

Ve lo raccontiamo per immagini, ben sapendo che già nel prossimo appuntamento lo spartito cambia.

Si comincia con l’occupazione casuale di un posto al bancone della cucina e alla scelta del vino di accompagnamento. Il vino spumante La Matta 2012 della cantina Casebianche è omaggio al contiguo territorio del Cilento. Appena prende aria restituisce le note fresche e poco alcoliche del Fiano rifermentato in bottiglia come facevano i contadini. Vino matto come matti sono Betty Iorio e Pasquale Mitrano che lo idearono nel 2010 dopo aver lasciato lo studio di architettura napoletano nel 2000. Una bottiglia a 2 anni dalla prima etichetta che ha retto magnificamente i 14 anni. Difficile trovarlo di tale vetustà, ma andrà benissimo anche una recente annata. E anche sui tortelli con l’astice.

L’entrée è di quelle che potrebbero circolare in un matrimonio tra Acciaroli e Napoli. Piatto di salumi e formaggi con pizza cilentana. La pizza dimostra che uno chef può fare il pizzaiolo. Più complicato che un pizzaiolo faccia lo chef. Sugosa come vuole la tradizione dei matrimoni in una trattoria cilentana del tempo che fu.

Peperone imbottito alla maniera napoletana. Camembert di bufala fatto in casa con rinforzo di mozzarellina del territorio. Salumi di San Gregorio Magno della mamma di Martina che lavora da Somnium. Plus l’insaccato conservato nella sugna e anti spreco: si mangia anche il budello.

Se fosse possibile polpettizzare un intero menu da matrimonio, la strada sarebbe segnata da questa polpetta. Il bicchiere in oro giustamente è da trattoria a festa per il matrimonio.

Polpetta 2, la distopica. Manzo con il suo fondo, purea di patate e senape in grani. Leggera come può essere la combo manzo – patate.

Non è una polpetta ma la lasagna cotta alla brace del Josper. Unico difetto, sa di poco: ce ne vorrebbe una mattonella.

Porzione giustificata dalla lasagna 2, la distopica. In menu, al vapore e poco invogliante sulla carta. Uno dei piatti migliori della serata. Sembra un gyoza aperto e mette insieme la suggestione orientale e la concretezza matrimoniale. Una lasagna al vapore orientale è per sempre.

Potrebbe mai mancare una pasta ripiena nel menu del matrimonio di una trattoria? Certo che no. Direttamente dagli anni ’80 (2980) i tortellini, panna, piselli e prosciutto. Molto più buoni della descrizione e della foto.

Sfrionzola per stare leggeri che all’indomani si va ad arare per campi. Maiale e papaccelle in grado di mettere il turbo anche al più esile degli invitati di un matrimonio. Categoria ricostituenti.

Diventa leggiadro l’agnello alla brace addomesticato nel profumo ma non nel sapore. Con caponata di patate – pomodorini – cipolle.

Si potrebbe alzare bandiera bianca, ma c’è la Tarta de queso che si scioglie allo sguardo e poi resta compatta al morso. Buonissima.

Il dolce del matrimonio napoletano proposto dalla Trattoria del 3000 non poteva che essere una pastiera. Ma alla maniera della cassata siciliana.

PS. Altro appuntamento è con i 2 cervelli e le 2 stelle Michelin del ristorante guidato da Domenico Candela il 26 ottobre (17 portate, 250 €).

Somnium. V. Cupe Superiore, 18, 84025 Eboli SA. Telefono: 347 781 0834. Instagram
